## How to read this report

Verified means checked by Exegra against this model revision within the stated scope and assumptions. Supported means resting on external evidence that Exegra did not derive from the model. A declared claim is not thereby true. A simulation of the model is not evidence about deployed software.

## Check and grounding

The check says how strong the evaluation behind a status is (holds-by-construction and restated-scenario cannot fail on a correct model; single-trace runs hand-written scenarios only); the grounding says whether what it reads is the platform's own entities (bound) or only its own inputs (self-contained). ## refresh-follows-commit

Every projection refresh is preceded by a delivered transaction commit: views are refreshed from a committed model, never from a candidate.

- Kind: temporal
- Method: precedes
- **Status**: Verified within the stated model and assumptions
- Check: single-trace: Decided by running 2 hand-written scenarios ("visual-edit-flow", "source-edit-flow"), with no exhaustive exploration and no refinement: a check of these traces, not of all executions.
- Grounding: self-contained: The behaviors the check reads ("interaction-flow", "transaction-flow", "session-flow", "projection-flow", "renderer-flow") refine nothing, are stored in no record, take no contract's events and read no anchored behavior; they decide on payload fields the scenarios supply (event "transaction-committed" field "revision", event "projection-refreshed" field "revision", event "refresh-requested" field "revision").

- Scope: Under fault model Local workstation (5 failure modes; bounds: at most 1 failures at once). Checked the events delivered and transitions fired in the traces of scenarios visual-edit-flow (11 steps), source-edit-flow (11 steps). This is a check of these traces, not of all possible executions. An occurrence counts as produced by the system when a transition fired or emitted it; a delivery from the scenario script does not, and an ordering between two scripted deliveries is not reported as verified.
- Explanation: Every occurrence of the "then" matcher had an earlier occurrence of the "first" matcher in each of the 2 traces of "visual-edit-flow" and "source-edit-flow". Each of its 1 negative violated it: "visual-edit-flow" with refresh-before-commit at step 3. Other executions, faults or inputs were not examined. The result holds under fault model "local-workstation" only as far as the listed scenarios inject its failure modes: they inject 0 of its 5; the rest were not exercised.

**Holds only if**

- `machines-abstract-the-code`: The scenario state machines in this file faithfully abstract the code they describe: session.apply, Project.apply and its commit, the session's onCommit and flush, the renderer, inspector and source editor refresh and the workspace write.

## save-follows-refresh

Every write request to the file service is preceded by a projection refresh: the write is asynchronous and happens after the views were refreshed, in all three edits that commit.

- Kind: temporal
- Method: precedes
- **Status**: Verified within the stated model and assumptions
- Check: single-trace: Decided by running 3 hand-written scenarios ("visual-edit-flow", "source-edit-flow", "edit-with-disk-conflict"), with no exhaustive exploration and no refinement: a check of these traces, not of all executions.

This is a check of these traces, not of all possible executions. Declaration order decided which transition fired: "workspace-detect-conflict" fired where "workspace-write" (unguarded, also enabled) is declared after it on event "save-requested" in behavior "workspace-flow". - Explanation: Every occurrence of the "then" matcher had an earlier occurrence of the "first" matcher in each of the 3 traces of "visual-edit-flow", "source-edit-flow" and "edit-with-disk-conflict". Each of its 1 negative violated it: "visual-edit-flow" with save-before-refresh at step 1. Other executions, faults or inputs were not examined. The result holds under fault model "local-workstation" only as far as the listed scenarios inject its failure modes: they inject 1 of its 5; the rest were not exercised.

- `machines-abstract-the-code`: The scenario state machines in this file faithfully abstract the code they describe: session.apply, Project.apply and its commit, the session's onCommit and flush, the renderer, inspector and source editor refresh and the workspace write.

Checked the events delivered and transitions fired in the traces of scenarios rejected-edit (6 steps, faults: validation-fails). This is a check of these traces, not of all possible executions. Declaration order decided which transition fired: "validator-reject" fired where "validator-accept" (unguarded, also enabled) is declared after it on event "model-parsed" in behavior "validator-flow". Swapping the declaration order of those transitions would change these traces.
- Explanation: The forbidden occurrence did not happen in each of the 1 trace of "rejected-edit". Each of its 1 negative violated it: "rejected-edit" with rejection-refreshes at step 9. Other executions, faults or inputs were not examined. The result holds under fault model "local-workstation" only as far as the listed scenarios inject its failure modes: they inject 0 of its 5; the rest were not exercised.

**Holds only if**

- `machines-abstract-the-code`: The scenario state machines in this file faithfully abstract the code they describe: session.apply, Project.apply and its commit, the session's onCommit and flush, the renderer, inspector and source editor refresh and the workspace write.

**Counterexample**

None.

**Evidence**

None is attached.

## conflict-never-overwrites

In the edit whose write conflicts with the disk, at every step, once the conflict is flagged the revision persisted on disk is still the initial one: the conflicting file is never overwritten.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: state-invariant
- **Status**: Verified within the stated model and assumptions
- Check: single-trace: Decided by running one hand-written scenario ("edit-with-disk-conflict"), with no exhaustive exploration and no refinement: a check of these traces, not of all executions.
- Grounding: self-contained: The behaviors the check reads ("workspace-flow") refine nothing, are stored in no record, take no contract's events and read no anchored behavior; they decide on payload fields the scenarios supply (event "save-requested" field "revision").
- Evaluator: exegra.trace/0.1
- Scope: Under fault model Local workstation (5 failure modes; bounds: at most 1 failures at once). Checked every configuration of the traces of scenarios edit-with-disk-conflict (11 steps, faults: disk-changed-externally). This is a check of these traces, not of all possible executions. Declaration order decided which transition fired: "workspace-detect-conflict" fired where "workspace-write" (unguarded, also enabled) is declared after it on event "save-requested" in behavior "workspace-flow". Swapping the declaration order of those transitions would change these traces.
- Explanation: The expression held in the initial configuration and after every step of each of the 1 trace of "edit-with-disk-conflict". Each of its 1 negative violated it: "edit-with-disk-conflict" with conflict-overwrites at step 9. Other executions, faults or inputs were not examined. The result holds under fault model "local-workstation" only as far as the listed scenarios inject its failure modes: they inject 1 of its 5; the rest were not exercised.

**Holds only if**

- `machines-abstract-the-code`: The scenario state machines in this file faithfully abstract the code they describe: session.apply, Project.apply and its commit, the session's onCommit and flush, the renderer, inspector and source editor refresh and the workspace write.

- `tests-exercise-shipped-modules`: The core tests import and run the same modules under src/core and tools that the application and the agent command line run.

## persistence-confined-to-workspace

Project names are validated and every path is resolved inside specs/<project>/ before any read or write; names and paths that escape that directory are refused, and generated files can only be written under generated/. The one exception is deliberate: a project.jsonc may name a specification file outside the project directory (a linked project, such as a specification kept in its own repository), which is then read and written there. project.jsonc is not writable through the workspace endpoints (write and import accept only spec.jsonc, layout.jsonc and evidence/results.json), so only a person editing the file on disk can point a project elsewhere.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: external-evidence

## trace-contract-conformance

## specification-copies-fresh-and-related

Every copy of a project's specification text that Exegra declares (the session model, the command line's project, the generated documents) states a freshness bound, and its holder is related to the specification file through declared relationships; the shared copy holds no principal data.

- Kind: structural
- Method: data-model
- **Status**: Verified within the stated model and assumptions
- Check: data-model: Decided over the records and copies of the model by the data rules.
- Grounding: bound: Reads every record and copy of the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.structural/0.1
- Scope: Checked the 30 records and 23 copies at this model revision against rules freshness-bound, holder-reaches-owner, no-principal-in-shared (items checked per rule: freshness-bound 23, holder-reaches-owner 45, no-principal-in-shared 2). Rule no-principal-in-shared read only the personal markers the model declares (a field's "personal" value or a reference to the principal): a field that holds personal data but declares neither is not seen, so the absence of a finding shows that the declared markers are absent from the copy, not that it holds no personal data.
- Explanation: No finding: Every copy states a freshness bound. A copy's holder is the owner of each record it copies, contains it or is contained in it, or a directed relationship path leads from one to the other. No shared copy holds a field that refers to the principal or is personal, or depends on authority (D-136).

**Holds only if**

None.

None is attached.

## crash-instance-loses-state

A crash-instance fault stops exactly the named instance at its time: its incarnation is incremented, its timers removed, its waiting requests removed with their timeouts, its in-service requests ended with their service-done entries and its units released, its restart drawn, its notify event broadcast; every targeted entry created for the old incarnation, a reply to a call it made before the crash included, is dropped as stale; a routed entry decided for it is ignored by that behavior only, and a broadcast skips it; instance aggregates exclude it while it is down; round-robin, random, any and least-busy routes skip it; a restart edge restores only its own incarnation; a crash of a missing or already down instance does nothing; and on restart the instance is in its initial state with its initial variables.

## poisson-arrivals-by-inversion

## request-lifecycle-defines-outcomes

Every request of a class follows the request lifecycle of docs/simulation.md: it starts when its start entry is first taken as head; it ends once, at the first delivered end event with its key (a deliver step that does not fail, whether or not a transition fires), as error at once when its start entry is dropped, crashed, stale or handled by no transition, or as timeout on a closed-client timeout; it is censored otherwise; only ok requests are finite samples; and every sample count, bucket power and derived metric is computed from these outcomes.

## runtime-data-copies-fresh-and-related

Every copy of Exegra's runtime data that this specification declares (session model and claim results, scene, simulation run, browser preferences and camera memory, command-line outputs, generated documents, bundles, proof outputs and the production bundle's embedded specifications) states a freshness bound, its holder is related through declared relationships to the owner of every record it copies, and no shared copy holds principal data.

- Kind: structural
- Method: data-model
- **Status**: Verified within the stated model and assumptions
- Check: data-model: Decided over the records and copies of the model by the data rules.
- Grounding: bound: Reads every record and copy of the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.structural/0.1
- Scope: Checked the 30 records and 23 copies at this model revision against rules freshness-bound, holder-reaches-owner, no-principal-in-shared (items checked per rule: freshness-bound 23, holder-reaches-owner 45, no-principal-in-shared 2). Rule no-principal-in-shared read only the personal markers the model declares (a field's "personal" value or a reference to the principal): a field that holds personal data but declares neither is not seen, so the absence of a finding shows that the declared markers are absent from the copy, not that it holds no personal data.
- Explanation: No finding: Every copy states a freshness bound. A copy's holder is the owner of each record it copies, contains it or is contained in it, or a directed relationship path leads from one to the other. No shared copy holds a field that refers to the principal or is personal, or depends on authority (D-136).

**Holds only if**

- `relationships-match-imports`: The relationships declared in this file match the real imports and calls of the code.

None.

**Evidence**

None is attached.

An occurrence counts as produced by the system when a transition fired or emitted it; a delivery from the scenario script does not, and an ordering between two scripted deliveries is not reported as verified.

Each of its 1 negative violated it: "app-start-and-open" with session-started-before-engine at step 1. Other executions, faults or inputs were not examined. The result holds under fault model "local-workstation" only as far as the listed scenarios inject its failure modes: they inject 0 of its 5; the rest were not exercised.

## real-runs-replay-through-self-model

The events a running Exegra really produces (requests and answers of the persistence client with the development server's own events, change notices, engine-worker messages, commits, claim evaluations, simulator controls, preference and camera storage writes) are recorded with their requestId and a logical sequence number and replayed through the behaviors of the scenario live-session; every divergence from the self-model (an event the specification does not name, an event the model does not predict at that point, a predicted event no behavior accepts, a payload value that differs, a predicted event not seen within 200 recorded events or by the end of a closed stream) is reported as a failed check at its step and listed in the simulator panel's Live mode, and a real open, edit and save replays without one.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Supported by external evidence
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1
- Scope: No model evaluation was performed. This rests on 9 evidence entities (ev-replay-recorded-stream-clean, ev-replay-wrong-order, ev-replay-unknown-event, ev-replay-missing-response, ev-replay-bound, ev-replay-payload, ev-replay-not-allowed, ev-replay-incremental, ev-live-browser), of which 9 have a recorded execution result; the rest is author-entered.
- Explanation: Exegra holds a recorded result of executing ev-replay-recorded-stream-clean, ev-replay-wrong-order, ev-replay-unknown-event, ev-replay-missing-response, ev-replay-bound, ev-replay-payload, ev-replay-not-allowed, ev-replay-incremental and ev-live-browser: pass.

**Holds only if**

- `tests-exercise-shipped-modules`: The core tests import and run the same modules under src/core and tools that the application and the agent command line run.
- `browser-tests-run-real-application`: The browser tests drive the real application served by the Vite development server, against scratch copies of this specification and of the multiplayer test fixture (one pair per Playwright worker) (and, read-only, a project linked locally), in the locally installed Chrome.
- `recorded-results-reflect-current-code`: A recorded result of a test in evidence/results.json was produced by the code and the specification as they are now.

**Counterexample**

None.

**Evidence**

| Id | Kind | Ref | Supports | Recorded |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ev-replay-recorded-stream-clean | test | tests/core/replay.test.js#a recorded open edit and save replays through live-session without a divergence | supports | pass |
| ev-replay-wrong-order | test | tests/core/replay.test.js#an event in the wrong order is a divergence | supports | pass |
| ev-replay-unknown-event | test | tests/core/replay.test.js#an event the specification does not name is a divergence | supports | pass |
| ev-replay-missing-response | test | tests/core/replay.test.js#a predicted response that never comes is a divergence | supports | pass |
| ev-replay-bound | test | tests/core/replay.test.js#a predicted event not seen within the bound is missing even while the stream is open | supports | pass |
| ev-replay-payload | test | tests/core/replay.test.js#a recorded payload value that differs from the prediction is a divergence | supports | pass |
| ev-replay-not-allowed | test | tests/core/replay.test.js#a predicted event that no behavior consuming it accepts in its state is a divergence | supports | pass |
| ev-replay-incremental | test | tests/core/replay.test.js#the replay is incremental and deterministic | supports | pass |
| ev-live-browser | test | tests/browser/live.spec.js#live mode replays an edit and its save through the self-model on the right components without divergence | supports | pass |

## engine-run-terminates-proved

Every run of an engine input that the engine accepts stops: its outcome is never running, and it has at most stepBound(stepLimit) steps.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: lean-theorem
- **Status**: Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code
- Check: lean-theorem: A theorem of the Lean core, "Exegra.runInput_terminates", checked by the Lean kernel; it covers every input.
- Grounding: bound: States a property of the Lean definitions "Exegra.runInput".
- Evaluator: lean.kernel/4.35.0
- Scope: Checked by Lean 4.35.0 at 2026-11-19T04:01:25.506Z, as recorded: the theorem's proof re-checked by the kernel and its axioms collected. It establishes the statement about the Lean definitions it names. That the code which runs is those definitions rests on the trusted base: the Lean compiler and runtime, the WebAssembly toolchain and the JS glue (the claim's assumptions).
- Explanation: "Exegra.runInput_terminates" is a theorem; the Lean kernel accepted its proof again; it depends only on propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound; its statement is about Exegra.runInput.

**Holds only if**

- `trusted-lean-kernel`: The Lean 4 kernel of the toolchain formal/lean-toolchain pins (v4.35.0) accepts only well-typed proof terms.
- `trusted-lean-compiler-runtime`: The Lean compiler translates the Lean core faithfully to C, and the Lean v4.35.0 C/C++ runtime, with the two-hunk patch formal/wasm/patches/lean-v4.35.0-runtime-wasm.patch applied, executes that C as Lean defines it.
- `trusted-emscripten-toolchain`: Emscripten 7.0.0 (clang/LLVM, wasm-ld, binaryen, musl libc, libc++ and the generated JS loader) compiles and links the C of the Lean core and the runtime to WebAssembly (wasm64, Memory64) without changing its meaning.
- `trusted-libuv`: libuv v1.48.0, linked into the WebAssembly module because the Lean runtime references it, does not affect the result of a call: the core reaches none of its I/O.
- `trusted-js-glue`: The glue between JavaScript and the Lean core (src/core/lean.js, formal/wasm/glue.c, the emscripten-generated exegra-lean.mjs) passes request and reply bytes unchanged and reports failures as errors.
- `trusted-wasm-engine`: The WebAssembly engine of the host (V8 in Node and Chrome, SpiderMonkey in Firefox) executes the module according to the WebAssembly specification, Memory64 and exception handling included.

**Counterexample**

None.

None is attached.

## concordance-results-are-evidence

A linked project's Concordance results (concordanceResults in project.jsonc) become derived test evidence of their claims: a pass makes a requirement supported and never verified, a fail makes it violated, a claim without an entry stays not evaluated, and a results file for another tool or specification, or an entry for an unknown claim, changes no status. A results file that names a specSha256 (the sha-256 of the specification text it was computed from) is not applied when that differs from the current text; one that names none is applied with its revision recorded as unknown, and its explanation says so.

- Kind: structural
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Supported by external evidence
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1
- Scope: No model evaluation was performed. This rests on 8 evidence entities (ev-concordance-pass, ev-concordance-fail, ev-concordance-absent, ev-concordance-diagnostics, ev-concordance-unknown-claim, ev-concordance-unconfigured, ev-concordance-directory, ev-concordance-watched), of which 8 have a recorded execution result; the rest is author-entered.
- Explanation: Exegra holds a recorded result of executing ev-concordance-pass, ev-concordance-fail, ev-concordance-absent, ev-concordance-diagnostics, ev-concordance-unknown-claim, ev-concordance-unconfigured, ev-concordance-directory and ev-concordance-watched: pass.

**Holds only if**

No assumptions are declared.

**Counterexample**

None.

**Evidence**

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| ev-concordance-fail | test | tests/core/concordance.test.js#a Concordance fail makes a requirement violated | supports | pass |
| ev-concordance-absent | test | tests/core/concordance.test.js#a claim without a Concordance entry stays not evaluated | supports | pass |
| ev-concordance-diagnostics | test | tests/core/concordance.test.js#a Concordance file for another tool or specification changes nothing | supports | pass |
| ev-concordance-unknown-claim | test | tests/core/concordance.test.js#an unknown claim id is a warning and the other entries still apply | supports | pass |
| ev-concordance-unconfigured | test | tests/core/concordance.test.js#a project without concordanceResults is unchanged | supports | pass |

## core-logic-in-lean

Every implemented component of the verified kernel (tag verdict-kernel: the code that decides whether a claim holds, namely model derivation, the semantic hash, engine-input derivation, the engine, the explorer and temporal checker, claim verdicts and their helpers, and transactions) is implemented in Lean, so the claims about the kernel are theorems about the code that runs. Everything else (layout, projections, scene, generators, UI, live replay, request-log rendering, command-line arguments, workspace I/O, the JSONC text layer) is JavaScript outside the verdict path.

- Kind: structural
- Method: implementation-language
- **Status**: Violated
- Check: structural: Decided over the whole declared component graph by "implementation-language".
- Grounding: bound: Reads the declared components and relationships of the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.structural/0.1
- Scope: Checked the declared implementation.language of 14 implemented components at this model revision. The declaration is not compared with the files (the assumption that declarations match the code).
- Explanation: 4 of 14 implemented components are lean; 10 are not yet (js: expression-language, queries, transactions, claim-evaluator, simulation-contract, fault-model, contract-conformance, data-model, metric-claims; mixed: workload-model). Porting the rest is planned (WG-4785).

**Holds only if**

- `relationships-match-imports`: The relationships declared in this file match the real imports and calls of the code.

**Counterexample**

- Summary: Not yet lean: expression-language, queries, transactions, claim-evaluator, simulation-contract, fault-model, contract-conformance, data-model, workload-model, metric-claims.
- Entities: `expression-language`, `queries`, `transactions`, `claim-evaluator`, `simulation-contract`, `fault-model`, `contract-conformance`, `data-model`, `workload-model`, `metric-claims`

**Evidence**

None is attached.

## realisation-roots-traced

When project.jsonc declares realisation roots, traceability checks each module written <alias>:<path> against that root's directory, reports a module naming an undeclared alias as missing, and reports unclaimed files only for roots that declare include globs (minus exclude globs); with no roots declared, the traceability result is the same as without the feature.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Supported by external evidence
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1
- Scope: No model evaluation was performed. This rests on 1 evidence entity (ev-external-traceability), of which 1 has a recorded execution result; the rest is author-entered.
- Explanation: Exegra holds a recorded result of executing ev-external-traceability: pass.

**Holds only if**

- `tests-exercise-shipped-modules`: The core tests import and run the same modules under src/core and tools that the application and the agent command line run.
- `recorded-results-reflect-current-code`: A recorded result of a test in evidence/results.json was produced by the code and the specification as they are now.

**Counterexample**

None.

**Evidence**

| Id | Kind | Ref | Supports | Recorded |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ev-external-traceability | test | tests/core/external-realisation.test.js#traceability | supports | pass |

## external-not-run-distinct-from-fail

External test evidence is recorded as fail only when a matched JUnit test case reports a failure whose text matches none of the root's environmental patterns; a test absent from the report, skipped, reported as an error, or failing for an environmental reason is recorded as not-run, which `verify` never counts as a failure and the claim evaluator treats as having no result.

- Method: external-evidence

## integrity-rules-evaluated

Integrity rules are part of the specification and are evaluated on every model: each holds with the number of instances checked, or is violated with its counterexamples (bindings, entity ids, lines); an invalid rule is a validation error, a rule over nothing is reported vacuous, and every rule of the fixture can be made to fail by a stated mutation.

- Method: external-evidence

## integrity-proved-in-lean

exegra prove-integrity establishes a rule that holds by a kernel-checked proof by reflection (eval_iff, decide +kernel) and a violated rule by a kernel-checked refutation from each concrete witness (refutes_sound), with Lean's standard axioms only, and reports a rule incomplete when the Lean checker and the JavaScript evaluator disagree.

## reader-pages-come-from-model

A reader page is derived from the model and its view alone: every row, count and section it shows is an entity of the model selected by the view's kind and conditions, grouped, classified and faceted by the view's rules, and a view without blocks reads the system, an index of its kinds and its first document.

## semantic-hash-ignores-presentation

The semantic hash of a model depends only on its schema version, its vocabulary and its top-level declarations (id, kind, data) up to order: source positions, JSON paths, nested declarations' records, references, expressions, the effective meta-model and the order of declarations do not change it.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: lean-theorem
- **Status**: Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code
- Check: lean-theorem: A theorem of the Lean core, "Exegra.Model.Model.semanticHash_presentation", checked by the Lean kernel; it covers every input.
- Grounding: bound: States a property of the Lean definitions "Exegra.Model.Model.semanticHash".
- Evaluator: lean.kernel/4.35.0
- Scope: Checked by Lean 4.35.0 at 2026-11-19T04:01:25.506Z, as recorded: the theorem's proof re-checked by the kernel and its axioms collected. It establishes the statement about the Lean definitions it names. That the code which runs is those definitions rests on the trusted base: the Lean compiler and runtime, the WebAssembly toolchain and the JS glue (the claim's assumptions).
- Explanation: "Exegra.Model.Model.semanticHash_presentation" is a theorem; the Lean kernel accepted its proof again; it depends only on propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound; its statement is about Exegra.Model.Model.semanticHash.

**Holds only if**

- `trusted-lean-kernel`: The Lean 4 kernel of the toolchain formal/lean-toolchain pins (v4.35.0) accepts only well-typed proof terms.
- `trusted-lean-compiler-runtime`: The Lean compiler translates the Lean core faithfully to C, and the Lean v4.35.0 C/C++ runtime, with the two-hunk patch formal/wasm/patches/lean-v4.35.0-runtime-wasm.patch applied, executes that C as Lean defines it.
- `trusted-emscripten-toolchain`: Emscripten 7.0.0 (clang/LLVM, wasm-ld, binaryen, musl libc, libc++ and the generated JS loader) compiles and links the C of the Lean core and the runtime to WebAssembly (wasm64, Memory64) without changing its meaning.
- `trusted-libuv`: libuv v1.48.0, linked into the WebAssembly module because the Lean runtime references it, does not affect the result of a call: the core reaches none of its I/O.
- `trusted-js-glue`: The glue between JavaScript and the Lean core (src/core/lean.js, formal/wasm/glue.c, the emscripten-generated exegra-lean.mjs) passes request and reply bytes unchanged and reports failures as errors.
- `trusted-wasm-engine`: The WebAssembly engine of the host (V8 in Node and Chrome, SpiderMonkey in Firefox) executes the module according to the WebAssembly specification, Memory64 and exception handling included.

**Counterexample**

None.

**Evidence**

None is attached.

- `trusted-lean-kernel`: The Lean 4 kernel of the toolchain formal/lean-toolchain pins (v4.35.0) accepts only well-typed proof terms.
- `trusted-lean-compiler-runtime`: The Lean compiler translates the Lean core faithfully to C, and the Lean v4.35.0 C/C++ runtime, with the two-hunk patch formal/wasm/patches/lean-v4.35.0-runtime-wasm.patch applied, executes that C as Lean defines it.
- `trusted-emscripten-toolchain`: Emscripten 7.0.0 (clang/LLVM, wasm-ld, binaryen, musl libc, libc++ and the generated JS loader) compiles and links the C of the Lean core and the runtime to WebAssembly (wasm64, Memory64) without changing its meaning.
- `trusted-libuv`: libuv v1.48.0, linked into the WebAssembly module because the Lean runtime references it, does not affect the result of a call: the core reaches none of its I/O.
- `trusted-js-glue`: The glue between JavaScript and the Lean core (src/core/lean.js, formal/wasm/glue.c, the emscripten-generated exegra-lean.mjs) passes request and reply bytes unchanged and reports failures as errors.
- `trusted-wasm-engine`: The WebAssembly engine of the host (V8 in Node and Chrome, SpiderMonkey in Firefox) executes the module according to the WebAssembly specification, Memory64 and exception handling included.

**Counterexample**

None.

## implementation-machine-readiness-rules

A behavior declared with level "implementation" is reported with an error for each rule it breaks, and for none when it breaks none: every variable has a resolving repr; instances are {key, storedIn} with key a key field of that record; the machine says which record holds it (storage {record, key} with key a key field of the record, or instances.storedIn, and the two agree when both are present); every variable is held in a field of that record (storage.fields, default the field of its name) whose type fits its repr, every storage.fields entry names a variable and a field of the record, and no field holds two things; a stored state has a required string or enum field (storage.state, default "state") whose values, through storage.stateValues when given, distinctly cover every state; a machine with storage.creates true fills every required field of its record, while a non-creating machine is not held to this; every event field read is a declared payload field and every payload type resolves; every emit names its receiver and its event a delivery; a transaction is declared; no transition is faultOnly; every elapsed event is emitted by a timer; transitions with the same state and event are explicitly ordered (guard or otherwise on the last, otherwise only on the last); every non-terminal state has an outcome or an ignore for every event of the machine. The rules are the ready.* codes of docs/semantics.md; a machine is keyed by the whole key of its record, every key field in order, in storage.key and in instances.key (storage.key-whole, WG-4907).

- Kind: invariant
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Supported by external evidence
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1
- Scope: No model evaluation was performed. This rests on 12 evidence entities (ev-readiness-rules, ev-readiness-fixed, ev-readiness-model-level, ev-readiness-shipped-projects, ev-readiness-storage-alone, ev-readiness-storedin-alone, ev-readiness-stateless-exempt, ev-readiness-non-creating, ev-readiness-storage-defaults, ev-readiness-field-fit, ev-storage-key-whole, ev-storage-key-whole-list), of which 10 have a recorded execution result; the rest is author-entered.
- Explanation: Exegra holds a recorded result of executing ev-readiness-rules, ev-readiness-fixed, ev-readiness-model-level, ev-readiness-shipped-projects, ev-readiness-storage-alone, ev-readiness-storedin-alone, ev-readiness-stateless-exempt, ev-readiness-non-creating, ev-readiness-storage-defaults and ev-readiness-field-fit: pass. Test evidence "ev-storage-key-whole" and "ev-storage-key-whole-list" has no recorded result.

## implementation-properties-declarative

The implementation-level properties (level, instances.storedIn, delivery, elapsed, an emit effect's to, transaction, otherwise, ignores) never change simulation: the engine input derived by simulationInput does not contain them and a specification that uses them parses and validates like any other. The one exception is the declared types of an implementation-level behavior (variable repr, payload field types), which reach the engine input only as the behavior's lowered types, never as repr.

## refines-checks-machine-refinement

A refines claim is verified only when, in the traces of the scenarios that run the implementation behavior, every initial configuration and every fired transition of it maps through its refinement mapping to the step its model machine takes or to a stutter, and at least one step matched a model transition; a step to the wrong model state, a step whose model guard is false, a wrong mapped post-variable, a moving step mapped to a stutter, different emitted events and a wrong initial state each make it violated, with the failing step as the last step of the counterexample trace. Timers the implementation sets are compared with the model's timers when the model transition sets some and are internal to the implementation otherwise; a model guard or effect that reads another behavior reads it from the same trace, through the machine that refines that behavior when it does not run itself. With refinement.timeScale, the model reads the trace time divided by it and its timer delays are compared multiplied by it. A delayed emit of the model is realised only by a delayed emit of the implementation for the same event and the payload fields the model sets, delivered after the model delay times the time scale; a timer never realises an emit, and an undelayed emit for a delayed one, or the reverse, is violated (reason emits).

- Kind: invariant
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Supported by external evidence
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1
- Scope: No model evaluation was performed. This rests on 22 evidence entities (ev-refines-verified, ev-refines-wrong-state, ev-refines-guard-false, ev-refines-post-variable, ev-refines-false-stutter, ev-refines-initial, ev-refines-emits, ev-refines-declaration-order, ev-refines-timer-same, ev-refines-timer-internal, ev-refines-timer-differs, ev-refines-cross-refined, ev-refines-cross-direct, ev-refines-cross-violated, ev-refines-time-scale, ev-refines-time-scale-violated, ev-refines-time-scale-absent, ev-refines-delayed-emit-scaled, ev-refines-delayed-emit-event, ev-refines-delayed-emit-delay, ev-refines-delayed-emit-at-once, ev-refines-timer-not-emit), of which 22 have a recorded execution result; the rest is author-entered.
- Explanation: Exegra holds a recorded result of executing ev-refines-verified, ev-refines-wrong-state, ev-refines-guard-false, ev-refines-post-variable, ev-refines-false-stutter, ev-refines-initial, ev-refines-emits, ev-refines-declaration-order, ev-refines-timer-same, ev-refines-timer-internal, ev-refines-timer-differs, ev-refines-cross-refined, ev-refines-cross-direct, ev-refines-cross-violated, ev-refines-time-scale, ev-refines-time-scale-violated, ev-refines-time-scale-absent, ev-refines-delayed-emit-scaled, ev-refines-delayed-emit-event, ev-refines-delayed-emit-delay, ev-refines-delayed-emit-at-once and ev-refines-timer-not-emit: pass.

## construct-consistency-rules

The cross-cutting constructs are checked as errors whether or not the specification sets system.generation: every policy, quota, retry, audit and validation names exactly one target; a validation names a declared input or field, carries exactly the parameters of its rule, fits the class of its target, and has a valid pattern and a non-empty range; a retention states exactly one of keepFor and until, a duration, fields only and always when it anonymises, declared fields, archiveTo only when it archives, and at most one per record; a quota has a positive limit, a duration window, and a declared contract error exactly when it refuses calls of a contract; a retry has positive attempts, durations, a cap only on an exponential backoff and not below the base, and a declared idempotency key; an audit captures declared fields once each, a chain field of bytes or string exactly when chained by hash; every condition, custom rule and capture parses, reads only what is in scope and types against the declared fields; and only a requirement has realisedBy.

## ui-consistency-rules

## run-time-timer-delays

A timer delay read at run time, {expr, simulate}, appears only on an implementation-level machine, its expr is an expression of a number over the machine's variables, time and the event payload, and it states the delay a simulated run uses; simulation and refines run the timer with that simulated delay, so refines compares the timer's event and payload and a model's numeric delay against the simulated one.

## wire-naming-and-json

system.generation.wireCase and a contract's binding.case accept exactly snake_case, kebab-case and camelCase, any other value being property.enum at its position; json is a built-in type that resolves wherever a type is named but is never the representation of an id or scalar (type.repr-json); in expressions a json value is its own class, compared only with == and != against json or null, refused by ordering, arithmetic, concatenation and boolean operators, by toasts and form controls, and no path reaches into it.

## ui-client-constructs-fourth-set

A contract input is encoded only as its encode allows: trim, split and as read a text input, split names a separator and is not combined with as, and empty is given only for an optional input. A form field takes an initial value only from a path reaching a value its control edits, or from the first item of a list of structs whose field equals an expression of the field’s class, and an always-derived field takes none; normalize is only on a text, textarea or password control. A form’s second call reads the first call’s response, and the outcome reads the outputs of both calls. onSuccess.view names outputs of the view model of the screen the success opens, each of its output’s class. onError prefix and announce, when given, are not empty; confirmAnnounce belongs to an action with a confirm and is not empty; alsoOn offers an action on the screens of one authentication only and reads nothing of one screen. An error message is well formed: its placeholders fit its kind, the kinds it words are errors some contract of its forms and actions lists, no two messages word the same thing for the same forms and actions, and a specification with a form or an action has a default message for refusals (a readiness gap when generation is set).

## telemetry-logging-rules

system.telemetry declares services with distinct ids and relative log paths (telemetry.service); an event's logging is {logged: true, service, level, name?, tenant?, fields} or {logged: false, reason} (event.logging-shape), names a declared service (event.logging-service) and a dotted lower-case log name unique within the service (event.logging-name), and logs only payload fields that exist (event.logging-field-unknown), are scalars (event.logging-field-type), are neither marked sensitive nor of a type that is sensitive through structs, lists and options (event.logging-field-sensitive), and are written under lower-case names that are not ts, level, service, event, correlation or tenant and not twice (event.logging-field-reserved); with system.generation every domain event states its logging (ready.event-logging-missing); an expectLog step names a logged event and its service (uiScenario.log-event) and expects only the line's tenant and allowlisted members (uiScenario.log-field); and a logged domain event is not reported as an unused event, since the running system emits it.

## union-map-types-rules

A type may be a tagged union (variants that are unit, struct-like or newtype, written in serde's external, internal or adjacent form, with the variant names in their own case) and a type text may be map<K, V>; validation refuses a union without variants or with a variant twice (type.union-variants), a variant field that does not resolve (type.union-field), an encoding whose tagField or contentField does not fit its tag or its variants (type.encoding), a map key that is not a string, an integer or an id or scalar over one (type.map-key), a struct that contains itself through struct fields alone (type.recursion) while allowing a cycle through a union variant, list, option or map, and a constraint (pattern, minLength, maxLength) on a type that does not take it or with an invalid regular expression (type.constraints); a union and a map value are opaque (class json) in guard expressions, and readiness (ready.*-undeclared) covers map types.

## language-construct-rules

A specification may state an expression language its users write (section languages, entity language): an AST union and a value union, shapes, scopes (record-scope with system attributes, param, constant, role-test) and operators, each with its syntax (infix, keyword-infix, prefix-word, call, literal or scope form, token, precedence, associativity), operand and result shapes, null handling and one primitive of a fixed catalogue whose semantics are pinned to the Strata kernel (crates/kernel/src/expr.rs); validation refuses two operators of one name (language.operator-duplicate), a word or symbol claimed twice (language.token-clash), a token of the wrong lexical class or a form the primitive is not written in (language.syntax), a missing or misplaced precedence or two associativities at one level (language.precedence), a primitive outside the catalogue (language.primitive-unknown), operands that disagree with the primitive arity or the AST variant (language.arity), an undeclared shape (language.shape-unknown), null handling other than the primitive's (language.nulls), an operator for a variant or case the AST does not have or a second operator for one (language.variant), and a scope that is malformed, missing for an operator or read by none (language.scope); with system.generation set an AST variant or case without an operator and an operator without a primitive are errors (ready.language-variant-uncovered, ready.language-primitive-missing).

## function-construct-rules

A specification may state pure functions (section functions, entity function): typed params, a returns type, an optional errors union, lets and a body in the function body language, which is the guard language extended with struct field access, if, let, match over unions, options, results and enums, struct and union constructors, list literals, the operations map, filter, find, any, all, fold, sortBy, take, concat, keys, values, get, insert, remove and format, eval and check of a declared language, call of another function, fail and try; validation refuses a body or let that does not parse (function.expr-syntax), a name, field, variant, type, function, language or operation that does not exist (function.expr-reference), an expression that is ill typed or a body that does not fit the return type (function.expr-type), a match that misses a case without _, has a case twice or an arm after _ (function.match-exhaustive), a call cycle through two or more functions, or a call of a function of itself on anything but a strict sub-value of a union parameter at one position shared by all its calls of itself (function.recursion), errors that are not a union or a fail or try outside that union (function.error-unknown), malformed or duplicate parameter and let names and parameter or return types that do not resolve (function.param), and an example that is malformed or does not evaluate to its result or error in Exegra's JavaScript evaluator (function.example-failed); with system.generation set a function without a success example or without an example of an error variant it can fail with is an error (ready.function-example-missing).

## set-tuple-nested-types-rules

A type text may be set<T>, a JSON array of distinct items in order, which resolves, keys maps and closes recursion as list<T> does and has the class list in guard expressions; a scenario value of a set type that repeats an item does not have the type; a union variant may be a tuple variant (tuple: true, two or more positional fields written as a JSON array in order, as serde writes {"Cmp": ["Eq", a, b]}); generic type texts nest (map<K, map<K2, V>>, list<map<K, V>>). Validation refuses a set of an unresolved type (type.field-unresolved, type.union-field), a map keyed by a set or a bad key inside a set or another map (type.map-key), a tuple variant with fewer than two fields, also marked newtype, or with an optional field (type.union-variants), and a tuple variant under an internal tag (type.encoding). A shared<T> is a sharing hint, identical to T on the wire: it resolves, keys maps, closes recursion, has the class and holds the values of T, and validation refuses a shared of an unresolved type (type.field-unresolved) and a struct that holds itself through a shared field (type.recursion).

## contract-errors-on-the-wire

A contract error states its HTTP status as status (an integer from 400 to 599, contract.error-status) or as kind (a key of system.generation.errorKinds, contract.error-kind-unknown), never both (contract.error-status-kind), and otherwise takes the default system.generation.errorKind or errorStatus; errorKinds map to statuses from 400 to 599 (generation.error-kinds) and the defaults are well formed and exclusive (generation.error-status); system.generation.errorBody names a struct type whose every field but code, message and correlation is optional (generation.error-body-type), a code field of an enum holding every kind and string message and correlation fields (generation.error-body-field, generation.error-body-code), needs errorKinds (generation.error-body-kinds) and maps exactly the six runtime failures to declared kinds (generation.error-body-runtime); when generation is set an error of a served contract with no status is ready.error-status-missing, and under an error body one with no kind is ready.error-kind-missing.

## machine-realisation-and-durability

## route-ambiguous-rule

Two screens whose routes tie are refused with route.ambiguous: both routes are non-catch-all, have the same number of segments, agree at every position (equal literals, or a parameter on either side), and have the same number of literal segments. Screens whose routes do not tie that way are not refused.

## contract-exposure-and-auth

A contract states its exposure (public, internal or local) and its auth as {scheme, ...} with scheme anonymous, session, service, provisioning-token, signature, signed-address, delegation or in-process; with system.generation set a missing or prose auth is contract.auth-structured and a missing exposure contract.exposure, and always an object auth has only the members of its scheme (contract.auth-structured) and in-process is exactly the scheme of local contracts (contract.exposure); a local contract has no HTTP binding and an internal one none under the gateway prefix (exposure.gateway); a public contract not marked readOnly needs a session or visitor with csrf and origin or a verified credential (auth.public-write); an anonymous public contract is readOnly or justified and limited by a quota, an anonymous internal one readOnly and justified (auth.anonymous); the service scheme is never public (auth.service-public); every named policy governs the contract (auth.policy-unknown); a development contract names a declared condition, is never anonymous and when public names roles (auth.dev-only); and a public contract states a tenantScope other than platform (contract.tenant-scope).

## derivation-construct-rules

A contract may be derived (realisation derived, with derivation {kind: pure, function} | {kind: read, needs, load, decide, and optionally the need loop insufficient and needRounds together} | {kind: command, needs, load, decide, commit, conflict, insufficient, needRounds, retries}, and optionally fault) or a facade (realisation facade, with a bffRoute); validation refuses a derivation on a contract that is not derived, a derived contract without one, an unknown kind, a field of another kind or a read naming only one of insufficient and needRounds (derivation.kind), a function whose parameters are not inputs (or load outputs) of the same name and type, whose result is not the outputs, whose error variants are not errors of the contract, an insufficient variant that is not a variant of decide carrying one need, or a fault that is not an error without payload (derivation.signature), a load contract that is a facade or itself, whose required inputs nothing fills, that takes the needs not exactly once, whose outputs are named like inputs or whose errors are not errors of the contract (derivation.load), the same for the commit contract with what decide gives, a conflict that is not a commit error, and an output that is neither a commit output nor a field of what decide gives (derivation.commit), need rounds (of a command or a read with a need loop) or retries missing or not non-negative integers (derivation.bounds), and a facade with implementedBy or whose bffRoute method, path and shape not exactly one served contract states (contract.facade-route); with system.generation set a derivation naming an undeclared function is an error (ready.derived-function-missing).

## function-language-kernel-fidelity

The function body language states what the Strata kernel computes without stand-ins: an AST field typed string or an id or scalar over string (has_role's role-id) is read as a name, so has_role evaluates and checks; a variant, enum value or field named like a keyword (value.null, expr.if, then, else) is written after a dot, as a match pattern and before a colon, and a keyword-named field is never bound by shorthand; the built-in unit type has the one value () (null in examples), and unit is not the representation of an id or scalar and cannot be declared again; print(L, e) writes an expression as the kernel's Display writes it; format's {:?} writes a value as Rust's derived Debug of the type specc generates and {:json} as serde_json's compact text (strings escaped, f64 as ryu writes it); startsWith, endsWith, length, trim, chars, snakeCase, parseTime and the ASCII character classes behave as the kernel's Rust (str::starts_with, chars().count(), str::trim over White_Space, transition.rs snake_case, plain.rs parse_time, char::is_ascii_*); a function calls itself only on a strict sub-value of a union parameter; and the language evaluator and checker give the kernel's results and exact error texts on the shared case table.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Inconclusive
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1
- Scope: No model evaluation was performed. ## capability-model-closed

Capability, plan, role, grant, delegation, tokenScope and featureFlag are entity kinds, and a contract auth names the capabilities it requires in requires; every capability a contract, plan, role, grant, delegation, token scope or flag names is a capability (capability.unknown); with system.generation set every contract that is not local requires at least one (capability.required); every capability is conferred by some role or grant and is core or entitled by some plan (capability.unreachable); a delegation passes on only capabilities its source (role, token scope, grant or delegation) holds and a grant confers only capabilities its issuer role holds (delegation.exceeds); and a grant or delegation maxDuration is a positive duration (capability.duration).

## example-fixture-rules

A specification may declare example fixtures (section exampleFixtures, entity exampleFixture: id, type, value, description) and a function example's argument, result or error may be {fixture: id} or {fixture: id, with: {dotted path: value}} instead of the value; Exegra resolves the reference, and the overrides in order, before it evaluates the example, so the example is checked exactly as if the value were written out. Validation refuses a reference to a fixture that is not declared (example.fixture-unknown); a fixture whose type does not resolve or whose value is not of its type, and a fixture that, with its overrides, is not the type of the parameter, result or error it stands for (example.fixture-type); and an override path that does not lead into the fixture's value through struct fields, map keys, list indexes and union variant names, an empty step, or a with that is not an object (example.fixture-override).

- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Inconclusive

## machine-transition-coverage

For every implementation-level machine whose realisation is shipped, exegra cover-machines reports, per machine, the transitions and guard outcomes (true and false of each guard) covered out of the total, read from the traces of the scenarios that run it; for each item they leave out it searches bounded sequences of the machine's input events with representative payloads and waits, keeps the runs that reach it as generated scenarios cover-<machine>-<n> whose traces exercise it, and writes them with --write as CreateEntity operations tagged generated-coverage that apply accepts and that, once applied, cover the same items; a transition reached by no scenario and no searched sequence is machine.transition-unreachable, a warning, or an error under system.generation.

- Kind: invariant

## function-branch-coverage

Exegra records which branches of each function its examples take (both branches of if, each arm of match, the value and the error path of try where the called function can fail, and fail), without changing what the examples evaluate to; exegra cover-functions reports per function the branches covered out of the total and each uncovered branch with its place in the body or let; with system.generation set a function with a branch no example takes is ready.function-branch-uncovered, an error, or a gap of severity info when system.generation.functionBranches is gap.

## product-layers-checked

A project's product files (project.jsonc "products") are validated together with the platform specification: a product that imports the platform and declares only the allowed sections passes, and each of these is refused with its own named rule, whose message names the offending id or section and says how to fix it: an imported id the platform does not declare (layer.import-unknown), a redefinition of a platform entity or of another product's (layer.redefines-entity), a section a product may not declare (layer.kind-not-allowed), a platform entity used without being imported (layer.import-missing), a reference nothing declares (layer.reference-unknown), a missing header (layer.header-missing), an import of another platform file (layer.import-mismatch) a reference into another product (layer.cross-product-reference), two product files with one product id (layer.product-id-duplicate), an entity id that fails the id pattern or is missing (layer.entity-id-invalid, layer.entity-id-missing) and a platform entity that names a product entity (layer.platform-references-product). A platform entity named under any reference key of the meta-model requires an import. Transactions edit only the platform text and refuse a product's entities (layer.product-entity).

- Kind: structural
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Inconclusive
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1

- Explanation: Only test evidence ("ev-layers-accepted", "ev-layers-cli", "ev-layers-import-unknown", "ev-layers-redefines", "ev-layers-redefines-product", "ev-layers-kind", "ev-layers-import-missing", "ev-layers-reference-unknown", "ev-layers-header-missing", "ev-layers-import-mismatch", "ev-layers-cross-product", "ev-layers-transactions", "ev-layers-product-id-duplicate", "ev-layers-reference-keys", "ev-layers-platform-references-product", "ev-layers-reload" and "ev-layers-header-version-platform") is attached and no result of running it is recorded, so Exegra cannot tell whether the test passes.

## browser-reloads-product-files

When a product file of the open project changes, is added or is removed on disk (or the products list of project.jsonc changes), the application's session re-derives its model from the product layers on disk: the workspace watcher reports the change as the file products, the session reads the project again, and the platform text and layout in memory are kept. A product file that does not parse keeps the last valid model.

## cli-registers-projects-from-scripts

A script can register a spec file (and product files) as a project in one command, and can run read-only commands on one without registering it: project add writes specs/<name>/project.jsonc with paths relative to the project directory, refuses an existing project unless --force, a name that is not a valid project name, a project that keeps its own spec.jsonc and files that cannot be read (writing nothing); project remove deletes the link; validate and the other read-only commands take --spec and --product inline and write nothing.

## product-layers-typed-sections

A product file may declare the typed sections objects, policies, queries, views, workflows, segments, settings, ledgerUnits, ledgerPools, ledgerLimits, mailTemplates, noticeTemplates, tokens, triggers, automations and operations, each value of which is checked against the platform type its section maps to (object-type-def, policy-def, query-def, view-def, workflow-def, segment-decl, setting-def, ledger-unit-decl, ledger-pool-decl, ledger-limit, mail-template-decl, notice-template-decl, token-decl, trigger-def, automation-def, tenant-operation-def): a value that does not have the shape of its type is refused with layer.value-invalid, a union value the platform union lacks with layer.outside-catalogue, and a name in the product namespace that no product entity declares with layer.reference-unknown or that another product declares with layer.cross-product-reference. The typed sections are not entities of the combined model and do not change the platform semantic hash. specc reads the same table and gives the same rule codes.

## product-layers-namespace

Every entity id of a product file begins with the namespace of its product, the product id with each hyphen replaced by an underscore followed by two underscores, and a local name; an id outside it is refused with layer.namespace-missing. A capability a product declares is confined to that product: another product file that names it is refused with layer.cross-product-reference, and a product capability is reported unreachable only when no role or grant confers it.

## product-layers-expressions

An expression written as text in a typed section of a product file (a policy condition, a trigger condition, an operation's check, a field default or read rule, a constraint, a segment predicate, an automation's expression) is parsed and checked with the tenant rule language in the scope it is written in: a text that does not parse, or uses a name or call the language lacks, is refused with layer.expr-syntax; a record field the object type of the scope lacks with layer.expr-unknown-field; a role neither the platform nor the product declares with layer.expr-unknown-role; a param the scope does not bind with layer.expr-unknown-param; and an operand of a shape its operator does not accept, a comparison of two shapes, or a hop through a field that is not a single reference with layer.expr-type. The nine operators of the language (checked + - *, a timestamp plus or minus milliseconds, ms_between, days, hours, minutes, concat, text) are parsed and typed as the language declares them, with the sign rule for a - before a digit. Each refusal names the product entity, the place and text, and the position in the text. specc gives the same rule codes.

- Method: external-evidence

- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.

## format-gaps-rules

A contract may be a target (realisation target: specified, not implemented, served by no route, never loaded, committed or lowered onto by a derivation or facade); a derivation of a read or command may state header: true (the load with no needs first, its outputs readable by the needs function, then the needs loaded in one round that no need round counts); an untagged union (encoding tag untagged) is written as the raw JSON of its variant, read in declaration order, and validation refuses one with a tagField, contentField or variantCase, two variants written as one JSON shape or a json newtype before another variant (type.encoding); binding.suppliedBy says an input comes from the route or session and never the body, and validation refuses one naming no input or no such supplier (contract.supplied-by), and a form field or argument (form.supplied-input) or action argument (action.supplied-input) that fills one; a function body may write the JSON null where a json value is expected and nowhere else (function.expr-type), and a union variant named like a keyword (plain-json.null) after a dot of a path or in a match pattern, eval may take a lookup that following a reference reads, and sortBy orders lists item by item and unions by variant and fields, as the kernel's Value does.

## language-checked-arithmetic

The primitive catalogue holds checked integer arithmetic (add-checked, sub-checked, mul-checked), time arithmetic (add-checked and sub-checked on a timestamp and an int, time-difference, days-ms, hours-ms, minutes-ms) and text (concat-checked, to-text), and the language evaluator gives for them the results and exact error texts of the shared case table: every integer operand and result lies in -(2^53-1) to 2^53-1, and outside it the evaluation fails with integer-overflow (never a wrapped, saturated or rounded value), at both ends of the range and for a product beyond 64 bits; a concatenation longer than 4096 bytes of UTF-8 fails with string-too-long; a null operand gives null; an operand of the wrong kind is the error <op> does not apply to a <kind>; and the checker refuses an operand of the wrong shape (a timestamp on the right of + or -, a timestamp under *, a list under text) with <op> does not accept a <Shape> operand, giving + and - the shape of their left operand.

## integrity-relations-least-model

For a program of derived relations that the Lean stratification check wf accepts and the checker solve solves, the solved table is exactly the least-model meaning Least: a tuple is in it iff it is in every interpretation that agrees with the lower strata and is closed under the clauses of its stratum.

- Method: lean-theorem
- **Status**: Not evaluated

- Explanation: Lean has not checked "Exegra.Integrity.least_eq_table": run "exegra verify" (or "exegra claims" where Lean is installed) to record the check.

## admit-never-writes-a-rejected-change

The admit command never writes the specification for a rejected verdict, even with --write, and reports every reason for the rejection (each claim that moved to a worse status, each new validation error, each new integrity violation, each new claim that is not verified or proved, a refused transaction), not only the first.

- Kind: structural
- Method: external-evidence

## admit-requirement-claims-pend-on-realisation

## pooled-quotas-executable

quotaPool is an entity kind and a scenario lists the pools it runs; a pool runs as one machine per pooled limit and modelled member, whose transitions admit usage on headroom (within the fair share when throttled), throttle a member over its fair share at the end of the stated consecutive windows unless its exemption applies from that window, release after the stated windows under, and record an exemption from the window it is set in or the next; simulation and the Lean explorer run these machines, so a claim over them is decided by exploration and a wrong rule (eligibility effective only from the next window, or a longer over-share window) yields a counterexample; and the pool rules are reported at their positions.

## machine-singleton-key

An implementation-level machine that creates its records (storage.creates), has no instances.key and is run by a scenario is reported with machine.singleton-key when a key field of its record has a type with no fixed value for the one instance the runtime creates at the start: only string, uuid or an integer, or an id or scalar over one, in a field that is not optional, has one. A machine keyed by instances.key, or whose key field has such a type, is not.

## specification-manifest-parts

A specification may be a manifest (specParts) listing part files, and Exegra reads it as the one document the parts make: array sections are concatenated in manifest order, a section that is not an array may be in one part only, and a missing part, a part that is itself a manifest, a manifest with sections of its own, a section two parts both hold and an id two parts give one section are refused with an error that names the files. The semantic hash is that of the one-file specification. exegra apply writes each changed entity back to the part that holds it, and a new entity to the part the manifest's specSections table names for its section (else beside its section, else the first part).
